巧新(1563 TT) Trading Buy

2026、 2027 年營運動能向上，投資建議 Trading Buy。  
投資建議 Trading Buy ：巧新(1563 TT) 努力進行轉型，半導體
新事業於 2H26 逐漸取得進展，且再生鋁出貨占比也明顯拉升，
帶動毛利率明顯改善。2026 年獲利表現回升且 2027 年獲利能
力續揚。惟考量近期汽車零組件族群評價不易拉升，投資建議
Trading Buy，目標價 76 元 (以 2027 年 EPS 及 PE 15 倍評價)。  
3Q26 本業獲利小幅增長，因業外收入挹注，整體獲利顯著季增；
預估 4Q26 獲利相對下降：3Q26 營收 20.16 億元，QoQ+1.83%、
YoY+18.48%。受惠於再生鋁採用比重提升且匯率相對有利，毛
利率由 2Q26 的 24.32%提升至 25.90%。但因運輸成本上漲，營
業費用同步增長，營業利益 QoQ+6.72%、 YoY+211.71%。因業
外認列大筆匯兌利益，稅率表現穩定。稅後純益 2.47 億元，
QoQ+247.56%、 YoY+478.06%， EPS 1.46 元。4Q26 方面，預估
營收 19.21 億元，QoQ-4.70%、 YoY+16.97%。獲利能力方面，
受惠於再生鋁採用率高，預估毛利率提升至 27.54%。因運費相
對下降，預估營業利益 QoQ+15.87%、 YoY+342.49%。因 3Q26
已認列大筆匯兌收益，若以保守角度看待 4Q26 業外貢獻，預估
稅後純益 1.67 億元，QoQ-32.69%、YoY 轉虧為盈，EPS 0.98 元。 
預估 2026 年獲利表現回升，2027 年獲利能力續揚：2026 年方
面，輪圈出貨小幅提升，而半導體相關零組件自 2H26 開始貢獻
初步營收。預估 2026 年營收 77.89 億元，YoY+11.63%。隨著再
生鋁採用率提升且營收規模增長，毛利率有望逐季提升，預估
2026 年毛利率提升至 24.97%，營業利益 YoY+43.13%。若業外
較無匯兌損失影響，預估稅後純益 6.16 億元，YoY+93.56%，
EPS 3.64 元。2027 年方面，根據車廠預示量，鋁圈出貨量將由
2026 年的 60 萬顆提升至 70 萬顆，有助於帶動車用產品營收增
長。而半導體營收占比有機會提升至 5~10%，將提供額外營收
動能。因再生鋁採用比重提升，且半導體產品毛利率相對較高，
2027 年毛利率及獲利能力有望優於 2026 年。預估 2027 年營收
92.77 億元，YoY+19.10%，稅後純益 8.56 億元，YoY+38.99%，
EPS 5.06 元。

2 群益投顧 
  巧新(1563 TT) ESG：  
Environmental 環境保護：(1)鋁下腳料 100%回收，製成再生鋁棒，持續循環
再利用。(2)導入各式節能設備具體節能，已降低溫室氣體排放量。(3)屏東
廠自建太陽能光電。Social 社會責任：(1)獲得 ASI(鋁業管理倡議)績效標準
認證。(2)定期進行人權盡職調查與評估，流程和政策涵蓋重要的人權問題，
保障員工和活動參與者的安全。(3)響應地方政府公益活動。Governance 公
司治理：(1)風險管理依公司程序辦理，內控嚴謹，並設有專職稽核部門定期
查核。(2)將誠信經營納入新人教育訓練課程與人力資源政策中，設立明確
有效之獎懲及申訴機制。(3)每年定期發布 ESG 報告書。

個股報告

6 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】 
  評等 定義 
  強力買進(Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間≥ 35%  
  買進(Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<35%  
  區間操作(Trading Buy) 5%≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<15%

中立(Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等 
預期近期股價將處於盤整 
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】 
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。  
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。 
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」、「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。 
停止推薦情境： 
1. 達目標價。 
2. 雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。 
3. 推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】 
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

Document 072: 2026年10月进出口数据点评：进出口同比延续高位增长，贸易顺差有所回落

### Page 1

事件点评

2026年 11月 12日

10月价格数据点评》-2026.11.11 
《修复的两张面孔：流动性先行，信
用再重估—固收专题》-2026.11.11 
《季节性因素影响下制造业 PMI回落
—2026 年 10 月 PMI 数据点评》
-2026.11.2  
进出口同比延续高位增长，贸易顺差有所回落 
——2026年 10月进出口数据点评

陈曦（分析师） 王帅中（联系人）  
chenxi2@kysec.cn 
证书编号：S0790521100002 
wangshuaizhong@kysec.cn 
证书编号：S0790125070016

事件：海关总署公布 2026年 10月进出口商品统计快讯，按美元计价，我国进口
同比+42.1%（前值为+31.2%，下同），环比-2.1%(+11.1%)；出口同比+35.2%
（+36.3%），环比-7.8%（+11.4%）；贸易顺差同比+18.5%（+49.7%），环比-21.2%
（+12.1%），进出口同比保持高位增长，环比增速均有所下降。 
⚫ 10月进出口数据关注点 
10月进出口同比均延续高增，增长速度且均达 30%以上，同比增长均远超市场
预期，受国庆假期影响，环比均较前值有所下降，进口增速高于出口增速，贸
易顺差有所收窄。出口同比+35.2%（+36.3%），进口同比+42.1%（+31.2%），进
出口金额均持续处于历史高位，进口同比 2026年以来均保持两位数增长，出口
同比增速连续两月保持 30%以上增速。Wind统计机构预测当月出口同比中位数
为+32.5%，平均数为+32.2%；进口同比中位数为+30.9%，平均数为 29.9%，10
月进出口同比均超市场预期，进出口 2026年以来始终保持高景气度，一方面是
由于全球 AI投资扩张，AI链上商品进出口高增拉动，另一方面国内“内卷”与
科技进步的共同推动下，中国商品的高性价比推动中国出口持续超预期增长。进
口超预期或主要与国际油价中枢抬升带动原油、成品油同比增速提升相关，出口
超预期与全球经济保持高景气有关，10月全球制造业 PMI为 52.6，保持扩张区
间。 
AI投资扩张仍是进出口高涨的最重要因素，价格上涨在其中起到的作用也不容
忽视。AI链上产品出口高增拉动出口增长，集成电路出口持续加速，10月出口
同比增长 137.6%。农产品、化工医药、劳动密集型商品等非 AI链上商品出口同
比均保持增长，分别增长 5.9%、34.0%和 17.5%，或主要源于 2025年同期基数
较低，且价格上涨起到的作用不容忽视，国际油价中枢持续攀升，价格上涨进一
步推动出口金额高增，但同比增长也一定程度上反应非 AI链条上商品出口的改
善。进口同比增速达 40%以上，AI产业链发展仍是最大助力，原材料价格上涨
一定程度上也起到助推作用。自动数据处理设备及其零部件 10月进口同比增长
261.7%，集成电路同比增长 78.5%。 
从出口的产品结构来看，出口产品保持分化态势，程度上有所收窄。AI相关产
业链发展推动集成电路、高新技术产品出口增速高增，带动整体增速提升，劳动
密集型商品出口持续修复。10月集成电路当月出口同比增长 137.6%，高新技术
产品同比增长 66.8%，均保持高速增长。劳动密集型商品 9月同比增长 22.8%，
10月同比增长 17.5%，依旧保持两位数增长，2025年基数的下降对修复带来一
定的支撑。我国货物贸易不断提质升级，出口由劳动密集型产品向高质、高技术
产品转变。 
从出口国家来看，10月我国对主要国家与地区的进出口基本均保持同比增长，
出口多元化是我国出口增速持续增长的动能所在。10 月对美国进出口同比均保
持高增，一方面与 2025年同期基数较低有关，也与科技公司资本开支增长下对
AI链上产品需求高增相关。从港口高频数据来看，10月重点港口完成集装箱吞
吐月度周均值同比增长 8.2%，高频数据与实际出口情况相符，一定程度上已提
前指明本次出口超预期增长。需要注意的是，11月、12月 2025年同期基数有所
抬升，或会对后续出口增速产生影响，但在全球经济保持高景气度的背景下，四
季度出口增速整体或仍具韧性。  
⚫ 债市观点：债券收益率或将上行，警惕债市的反转风险 
对于债市，我们认为债券收益率或将上行，警惕债市的反转风险。 
第一，市场大多观点认为，后续 DR001将围绕 1.25%运行，这存在落空的可能
性，或有利多出尽的风险。第二，金融时报连发两文指出“央行研究切换公开市
场操作主力品种，属于货币政策框架改革的一项举措，不同于通常讲的货币政策
降息加息…市场各方要避免将央行操作机制改革举措当作降息来炒作”，或指明
监管对当前债市的态度趋于谨慎。第三，拥挤度层面，当前公募等非银机构债券
久期已达近年高位，特别是经过这轮换锚发酵后，拥挤度再次提升，当前存在拥
挤度调整、估值回调风险。第四，基本面层面，从 9月经济数据来看，国内经济
有触底回升迹象，9月社融同比 2025年 9月正增，政府债发行速度加快，政策
逐渐落地下经济动能边际修复，当前债市的定价尚未反应基本面的边际修复，这
也是潜在的风险。 
⚫ 风险提示：政策变化超预期；经济变化超预期。

《证券期货投资者适当性管理办法》、《证券经营机构投资者适当性管理实施指引（试行）》已于2017年7月1日起正
式实施。根据上述规定，开源证券评定此研报的风险等级为R3（中风险），因此通过公共平台推送的研报其适用的
投资者类别仅限定为专业投资者及风险承受能力为C3、C4、C5的普通投资者。若您并非专业投资者及风险承受能
力为C3、C4、C5的普通投资者，请取消阅读，请勿收藏、接收或使用本研报中的任何信息。
因此受限于访问权限的设置，若给您造成不便，烦请见谅！感谢您给予的理解与配合。

本研究报告的署名人员具有中国证券业协会授予的证券投资咨询执业资格或相当的专业胜任能力，以勤勉的职业态
度，独立、客观地出具本报告，并对内容和观点负责。本报告清晰准确地反映了署名人员的研究观点，所包含的分
析基于各种假设，不同假设可能导致分析结果出现重大不同。本报告采用的各种估值方法及模型均有其局限性，估

股票投资评级说明
评级 说明
证券评级
买入（Buy） 预计相对强于市场表现 20%以上；
增持（outperform） 预计相对强于市场表现 5%～20%；
中性（Neutral） 预计相对市场表现在－5%～＋5%之间波动；
减持（underperform） 预计相对弱于市场表现 5%以下。
行业评级
看好（overweight）  预计行业超越整体市场表现；
中性（Neutral） 预计行业与整体市场表现基本持平；
看淡（underperform） 预计行业弱于整体市场表现。
备注：评级标准为以报告日后的 6~12个月内，证券相对于市场基准指数的涨跌幅表现，其中 A股基准指数为沪
深 300指数、港股基准指数为恒生指数、新三板基准指数为三板成指（针对协议转让标的）或三板做市指数（针
对做市转让标的）、美股基准指数为标普 500或纳斯达克综合指数。我们在此提醒您，不同证券研究机构采用不同
的评级术语及评级标准。我们采用的是相对评级体系，表示投资的相对比重建议；投资者买入或者卖出证券的决

定取决于个人的实际情况，比如当前的持仓结构以及其他需要考虑的因素。投资者应阅读整篇报告，以获取比较
完整的观点与信息，不应仅仅依靠投资评级来推断结论。

Lite-On Technology  | Asia Pacific
October  Sales +6% MoM/+32%
YoY
In this report, we focus on Lite-On's monthly sales, which we
believe could be a catalyst for its share price.
Details:
• October sales came in at NT$20,071mn (+6% MoM/+32% YoY), driven by the
server PSU/BBU and opto semiconductors (couplers and indicators for
industrial automation, new energy and AI applications).
• QTD revenue reached 33% of both MSe at NT$60,395mn (+5% QoQ/+36%

YoY), and consensus at NT$60,353mn (+5% QoQ/+36% YoY).
• By segment, Cloud & AIoT/Optoelectronics/IT&CE accounted for
66%/13%/21% of the monthly sales, respectively, implying growth of +19%/-
1%/-17% MoM and  +71%/+7%/-16% YoY.
Our view:
• We think its 4Q26 revenue is tracking slightly ahead of market expectations.
• We believe Lite-On will grow along with the AI capex cycle driven by its
power-related offerings, though execution and progress with new customers

remain key to watch.
• Stay EW.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Lite-On Technology (2301.TW)

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

### Page 1

Vanguard International Semiconductor Co

Robust 8" demand and pricing with solid 
12" fab ramp

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 250.00 TWD | Price: 132.00 TWD

3Q sales/margin supported by PMIC strength  
Vanguard's 3Q26 sales rose 10.2% QoQ on the back of increased wafer shipments (up 
5% QoQ), particularly for server and smartphone PMIC, and rising wafer prices (up 3% 
QoQ). As higher utilization of around 90% and a more favorable pricing and product mix 
more than offset rising manufacturing costs, the 3Q26 gross margin improved by 130bps 
QoQ, to 33.6%, bringing 3Q26 EPS to NT$1.62 (vs. BofAe/Street of NT$1.67/1.58). 
4Q capacity bottleneck offset by ASP uplift 
The company guided to 4Q26 sales growth of 5% QoQ at the midpoint, driven by a 1% 
QoQ increase in wafer shipments, with strong demand capped to some degree by tight 
capacity, and 4% higher ASPs QoQ, the stronger pricing reflecting rising costs. Assuming 
further utilization improvement above 90% amid this pricing strength, Vanguard expects 
the 4Q26 gross margin to expand to 35% at the midpoint, in line with BofAe.  
Solid 8" growth and smooth 12" ramp drive ’27-28 growth 
We believe Vanguard’s 8-inch utilization is on track to improve to the mid- to high 90s 
(%) in 1H27 and remain fully loaded from 2H27 on solid demand related to power IC for 
computing applications, while 12-inch VSMC should enter mass production from 1Q27, 
with utilization staying above 90% as capacity gradually expands to 44k WPM by 4Q28. 
Given the solid commitment from TSMC (interposer demand), the 12-inch margin profile 
should fare better than previously expected. This, coupled with an 8-inch price hike amid 
tight supply should drive GM expansion to 36% in 2027 (vs. 32.8% in 2026).  
Decent EPS CAGR of 72% supports high valuation  
Our EPS forecasts are largely unchanged at NT$6.17 in 2026, NT$10.33 in 2027, and 
NT$18.15 in 2028. We reiterate our Buy rating and PO of NT$250, still based on 17x 
2H27-1H28e EPS (vs. the stock’s historical 10-32x range) as a benign 8-inch supply-
demand landscape and smooth 12-inch ramp-up support a 45% sales and 72% earnings 
CAGR over 2026-28e.

Company Sector 
Semiconductors

Company Description 
Vanguard International Semiconductor Co is a pure 8-inch 
foundry headquartered in Hsinchu, Taiwan. The company 
has five fabs, one of which is in Singapore. Collectively, the 
capacity is now about 280k WPM, and the company focuses 
on fabricating power semiconductors and driver IC for both 
IDMs (integrated device manufacturers) and fabless 
customers.

Investment Rationale 
We have a Buy rating on Vanguard as we expect an 
improvement in the overall 8-inch foundry supply/demand 
landscape from TSMC's retirement of some legacy capacity 
and limited new industry capacity additions. The higher 
utilization and pricing upcycle in 8-inch, coupled with 
enhanced mix from market share gains in Power IC, are 
likely to support sales and gross margin expansion.

### Page 4

Price objective basis & risk 
Vanguard International Semiconductor Co (VGILF) 
Our PO of NT$250 is based on 17x 2H27-1H28 EPS. This target multiple is slightly 
below the midpoint of Vanguard's historical range of 10-32x, reflecting both 
depreciation associated with the Singapore fab and Vanguard's increasing share in the 
Power IC market amid a utilization and pricing uptrend.

Downside risks: 
(1) Higher cost burden if customers demand to move the production base to US and/or 
Europe. 
(2) Slowdown of growth in auto market where the company has 10-20% revenue 
exposure.

Upside risks: 
(1) Stronger-than-expected end market demand for power management IC (PMIC) and/or 
display driver IC (DDIC). 
(2) Expedited market share gain due to the order shift by customers from China-based 
foundries.

### Page 1

Summary: Merida posted its 3Q26 results on 11 November after market hours. Merida’s 
gross margin came in at 15.2% (vs. 19.8% in 2Q26; 13.7% in 3Q25), lower than our 
estimate (16.2%) and the consensus (17.3%). Its 3Q26 EPS was TWD1.06, lower than our 
forecast (TWD1.68) and the consensus estimate (TWD1.65). Merida plans to hold an 
investor call on 17 November and we will provide an update then.

We have a Buy (1) rating with a 12-month TP of TWD96, based on a target PER of 17x 
applied on our 1-year-forward EPS forecast. For more information on this company, please 
refer to our latest flash note, 2Q26 review: mainly dragged by Specialized, on 14 August.

What’s the impact 
• 3Q26 results missed. Merida’s 3Q26 revenue of TWD6,098m (-12.1% QoQ, -15.6% 
YoY) missed our and the Bloomberg consensus estimates by 13.6% and 11.1%, 
respectively, which we attributed to the discount when destocking the older models. Its 
gross margin came in at 15.2% (vs. 19.8% in 2Q26; 13.7% in 3Q25), lower than our 
estimate (16.2%) and the consensus (17.3%), due to less OBM sales and the price 
discounting for further inventory digestion. Its operating profit of TWD384m (-49.8% 
QoQ, -9.7% YoY) also missed our and the consensus estimates by 35.2% and 37.3%, 
respectively. Its operating margin came in at 6.3% (vs. 11.0% in 2Q26; 5.9% in 3Q25), 
missing our estimate of 8.4% and the consensus of 8.9%. Considering the equity 
investment loss of TWD150m+, its net profit of TWD176m (-44.8% QoQ, -71.9% YoY) 
missed our and the consensus by 65.0% and 65.6%. Its 3Q26 EPS was TWD1.06, lower 
than our forecast (TWD1.68) and the consensus estimate (TWD1.65).

range of 7-33x. Key downside risks: slower-than-expected e-bike penetration in the EU and 
the US, worse-than-expected demand in an inflationary environment and inventory digestion.

### Page 1

Micro-Star (2377.TW)
3Q26 Beat on Margins from ASP Pass-Through and Richer Mix
CITI'S TAKE 
MSI reported 3Q26 net profit of NT$4.0bn (+34% QoQ/+138% YoY), 
beating Citi/cons by 27%/45% on stronger-than-expected margins and 
non-op gains. GPM of 17.0% (+1.1ppts QoQ/+5.8 ppts YoY) topped Citi/cons 
at 15.8%/14.5%, as ASP hikes passed through rising component costs while 
moving-avg inventory costs lagged, alongside a richer high-end product 
mix. For 4Q26E, we expect further ASP increases and a rising server 
contribution to largely offset seasonal shipment declines. Into 2027, we 
expect PC demand to stay weak and pass-through room to narrow as 
memory price increases moderately. We raise 2026E-28E earnings and our 
TP to NT$145 (10x 2027E EPS). We stay Neutral as the share price already 
reflects the pass-through upside.
3Q26: Margin-led beat on cost pass-through and better mix — MSI reported 
3Q26 net profit of NT$4.0bn (+34% QoQ/+138% YoY), beating Citi/cons forecasts 
by 27%/45%, mainly on stronger-than-expected margins and non-op gains. GPM 
of 17.0% (+1.1ppts QoQ/+5.8ppts YoY) came in above Citi/cons at 15.8%/14.5%. 
Mgmt. attributed the upside to 1) ASP increase, as MSI raised prices in line with 
suppliers’ cost increases while its moving-average inventory costs rose less; and 2) 
a higher mix of high-end products. Non-op gains, including FX gains of over 
NT$100mn, further boosted the bottom line.
Price hikes to continue; 2027 PC market to keep shrinking — We reiterate our view 
that 4Q26 shipments will decline seasonally from the 3Q26 peak. However, mgmt. 
expects rising raw material costs and continued pass-through to keep lifting ASPs, 
offsetting lower volumes and making the seasonal decline milder than in prior years. 
The server business should also contribute more, potentially reaching a high-single-
digit revenue share. Into 2027, we expect weak PC demand amid elevated costs, 
with MSI’s MB and PC shipments declining by over 10%. Pass-through room should 
narrow as memory price increases moderately, though margins should stay elevated 
for a while, as mgmt. expects memory supply to stay tight in the coming quarters.
Implications — We raise our 2026E-28E earnings forecasts by 6-27%, reflecting 
sustained strong component cost pass-through and higher margin assumptions. 
We adjust TP to NT$145 (from NT$140), implying 10x 2027E EPS (from 12x). We 
believe the share price already reflects MSI’s opportunity to pass component costs 
through to end products, and we expect limited upside going forward, given that the 
PC demand recovery remains highly uncertain. Maintain Neutral.

Bull/Bear: Micro-Star (2377.TW)

### Page 6

Micro-Star (2377.TW)
13 November 2026 Citi Research
6
Micro-Star
Company description
Established in 1986 and listed in 1998, Taiwan-based MSI is a leading
OBM/ODM for gaming PCs, motherboards, graphic cards, industrial PCs, and
servers. MSI started business as a motherboard manufacturer but
transformed itself to become a leading gaming PC play in 2012 with a
comprehensive product portfolio across the gaming system and components.
MSI enjoys about 16% global share in the gaming NB industry, and is ranked

No.3 in graphic card shipments and No.3 in motherboard shipments globally.
Investment strategy
We rate MSI shares at Neutral as we anticipate earnings upward pressure
from the delince in PC and MB/VGA shipments in the near-term. While the
soaring component costs could be partially offset by price hike, we expect
muted demand and AI resource crowding to wiegh on margins upside. Further
upside maintains limited, though, until we see a meaningful recovery in core

gaming demand or a more substantial contribution from the server business.
Valuation
Our target price of NT$145 is based on 10x 2027E EPS, below the stock's five-
year average forward 12-month PE. This valuation reflects the earnings
downside risks from muted PC demand and margin pressure from soaring
component costs, while accounting for the time required for the server
business to achieve meaningful scale.
Risks
Key upside risks that could cause MSI's shares to trade above our target price

include: 1) a sharper-than-expected recovery in high-end gaming demand;
and 2) faster-than-anticipated revenue contribution from the AI server
segment. Key downside risks include: 1) a deeper-than-expected contraction
in global PC shipments; 2) prolonged supply shortages for gaming GPUs due
to Ai resource crowding; and 3) inability to pass through surging memory and
CPU costs to price-sensitive consumers.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

### Page 1

Idea
Alchip Technologies Ltd  | Asia Pacific
European NDR: strong five-year
outlook in AI ASICs; OW
Alchip's strong growth potential is rooted in US CSPs' AI ASIC
replacement cycle. Even after the recent rally, our price target
still implies 46% upside.
Right after its  3Q26 earnings call, we took Alchip's CEO, Mr. Johnny Shen, and CFO,
Mr. Daniel Wang, to visit European investors for discussion of the company’s long-
term strategy and outlook. Here are key takeaways:

Entering another phase of fast growth in the coming 4-5 years, with good
profitability: Alchip is sticking with its back-end design service focus, which
dovetails with CSPs' strategy of COT (customer-owned tooling). The long-term
trend is for CSP and AI lab customers to own the architecture and front-end design,
while pursuing efficiency in back-end design and production service. That strategy
can maximize the tokens generated per dollar  of AI capex. Besides winning future

generations with a major customer (AWS, 2nm and 1.4nm), Alchip has secured a
second big customer and is very close to winning a project with another US CSP (we
think it should be Google –  TPU computing dies). We project US$10bn total revenue
in 2028 –  4x our US$2.4bn estimate for  2026. Management thinks US$20bn
revenue p.a. by the end of this decade is possible, if the balance sheet permits.
More discussion of the second customer: The company still cannot disclose the

name of the second customer given the NDA, but reiterated that it expects a US$1bn
revenue contribution in 2027. Alchip provides part of the design service for the end
customer, and the gross margin is reasonably good. Although we don’t rule out the
possibility of another design service provider in this ASIC project for IP or front-end
design, we maintain our view that the end customer is a US CSP that uses Alchip for
back-end design service. Our industry checks don't see a CoWoS booking for this

ASIC, so we think it could be related to AI networking.
Getting close to winning a third big customer: In our October 11 report, Solid 2027
and 2028 outlook with potential catalysts, we already indicated that Alchip is a
strong candidate for Google TPU computing die back-end design service.
Management continued to show high confidence in a new project win without
specifying customer name; it expects some NRE revenue to come by the end of
2027. We asked which customer (second or third) could make a bigger revenue

contribution 2-3 years down the road – management thinks the third customer may
show bigger revenue potential.
Alchip's value underpinned by major customer (AWS): This could be reflected in
higher allocation of Trainium3 in 2027 and better business terms for Trainium4
(profits to grow with the volume upside). Management also expects 1.4nm design
activities to kick off some time in 2027 –  the key to five-year growth visibility.
Please see the following section for investors' feedback and our views.

### Page 2

Idea
2
Stronger revenue   guidance for 2027 and 2028
European investors' feedback
1. Alchip was somewhat overlooked in the AI ASIC market over the past two years,
compared with MediaTek and GUC, which continued to win new projects. Winning
second and third big customers is considered a re-rating driver – it mitigates the
concentration in a single customer (AWS).
2.  Meanwhile, European investors generally agree that AI ASICs should gain share
against GPUs, given tighter AI capex budgets. We share the view expressed in

recent comments from Alchip and MediaTek management: certain AI ASICs that
companies help to design provide very competitive TCO (total cost of ownership).
3. It is hard to track ASIC supply chain data points in real time, but the ASIC story
and Alchip's industry position can justify a long-term investment. Changes in
short-term data points wouldn't stop European investors from investing in Alchip.
4.  On valuation, investors think Alchip's 2028e implied P/E of 11x based on our EPS

estimate is relatively attractive vs. other global ASIC design service peers.
Considering this feedback, we think Alchip stock should  continue to re-rate: The stock
has been volatile, rebounding 50% since November 6 after a 40% pullback over October
12 to November  6. (The TAIEX was  +10% and -20% over those periods.) Based on our
estimates, P/E is now 17x for 2027 and 11x for 2028, lower than its historical average of
28x one-year forward P/E since 2019.

Key catalysts could be:
• November 30 –  AWS re:Invent conference with Trainium4 announcement;
• Supply chain identification of the second big customer's name and order potential;
• Confirmation of Google computing die back-end design service;
• Bigger Trainium3 revenue ramp in 3Q27 vs. 2Q27.

Price target remains unchanged at NT$5,888

income model. Our cost of equity of 10.4% (beta of 1.4, equity risk premium of 6.0%, and
risk-free rate of 2.0%) remains unchanged, as do our intermediate growth rate of  12.5%
and terminal growth rate of 4%.
Our bear case value of NT$2,465 and bull case value of NT$6,880 are also unchanged.

MS Rating
▪ We continue to believe that AI ASICs will

outgrow AI GPUs in the coming years, and
Alchip is one of the few ASIC design service
providers that can deal with increasingly
complex designs (at 2nm) with good
production/design track records for large
leading edge chips.
▪ AWS's announcement of its 3nm
Trainium3 chip matches the timing of
Alchip's 3nm project win. The growing
number of use cases for Trainium (e.g.,
Apple's adoption) bodes well for Alchip's
revenue growth in 2026.
▪ We believe Alchip has strong potential to

win Trainium4.
▪ Our price target implies 26x our 2027e
EPS, which we ﬁnd undemanding vs. our
>100% revenue CAGR for 2025-28e.

NT$6,880.00
30x 2027e EPS
AI chipsets and HPC projects start to
proliferate, amid substantial end demand.

Alchip's leading-edge tape-out numbers
increase signiﬁcantly, with some high-proﬁle
project wins at 3nm or even 2nm. Alchip is
able to secure enough foundry capacity
(front-end wafers and back-end packaging)
and substrates for all of its leading-edge
turnkey production. Alchip gradually gains
market share within the design service
market, amid continued proliferation of
custom chip demand.
NT$5,888.00
26x 2027e EPS
AI chipsets and HPC projects start to

proliferate amid strong end demand,
resulting in steady growth in tape-outs and
volume. There might be a slowdown in
Alchip's Chinese customers' design activity
for leading-edge chips as a result of US
export controls; we believe this would be
largely offset by ASIC demand strength
from non-China regions.
NT$2,465.00
11x 2027e EPS
AI chipsets and HPC projects lack concrete
end demand, resulting in delayed tape-outs
and mass production. Alchip's leading-edge

project pipeline deteriorates, or some
customers turn directly to TSMC. AI GPU's
dominance in AI computing compresses the
market share gain potential for overall ASIC,
with customers starting to slow their
custom silicon developments - and therefore
ASIC procurement plans.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

公司拜訪快報

力旺(3529)：2H26 權利金大幅成長、Security 長期機會大 
建議：維持買進

一、2026 年前三季權利金營收
+13.4%YOY，PUF-based 權利
金+927%YOY 
 2026 前三季權利金營收 21.49 億元，+13.4%YOY。 
成熟製程 NeoBit 佔 24.6%，+8.6%YOY。 
先進製程 NeoFuse 佔 70.7%，+12.3%YOY。 
PUF-based 佔 1.5%，+927%YOY。 
MTP 佔 3.2%，+32.5%YOY。 
權利金成長預計將持續加速，主要受惠於先進製程應用的 ASP 提升、客戶新產
品持續放量、PUF 相關部署的營收貢獻增加，以及高權利金費率應用的產品組
合比重提高。

二、2026 年前三季授權金
+38.2% YOY，PUF - based 
+234% YOY 
 2026 前三季授權金營收 12.51 億元，+38.2%YOY。 
NeoBit 佔 11.9%，-39.3%YOY。 
NeoFuse 佔 36.5%，+35.1%YOY。 
PUF-based 佔 31.7%，+234%YOY。 
MTP 佔 19.9%，+22.9%YOY。 
授權業務預期將維持強勁的成長動能，主要受惠於先進與成熟製程節點、硬體安
全、AI 基礎設施，以及次世代快閃記憶體 Flash 應用等領域。

三、維持買進理由  1) 受惠於先進製程應用的 ASP 提升、客戶新產品持續放量、PUF 相關部署的
營收貢獻增加，以及高權利金費率應用的產品組合比重提高，權利金成長預計將
持續加速。 
2) 授權業務預期將維持強勁的成長動能，主要受惠於先進與成熟製程節點、硬
體安全、AI 基礎設施，以及次世代快閃記憶體 Flash 應用等領域。 
3) PUF-based 已貢獻 2026 年前三季營收 12.6%，+252%YOY。應用面包括
CPU、Storage、光通、FPGA、高速傳輸晶片多面向。Arm Neoverse CSS V3
已將力旺的 PUF 列入核心元件。

Document 284: [329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사HD현대중공업(329180) 피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사HD현대중공업(329180) 피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung

### Page 1

(329180) 
HD 현대중공업 
2026.10.29

AI 로보틱스/방산/조선/운송 양승윤 
6139 / syyang0901@eugenefn.com

피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사 
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 610,000 원(하향) 
 현재주가 285,500 원(10/28)

 3Q26 매출액은 5조 7,536억원(+30.2%yoy), 영업이익은 8,992억원
(+61.3%yoy, OPM 15.6%)을 기록. 영업이익 컨센서스 9,792억원 하회.  
 평균 환율이 약 79원 하락하며 영업이익 약 450억원이 줄었고, 조업
일수는 47일로 통상 계절성 대비 4일 추가 감소해 약 500억원의 이
익 감소 효과 발생. 임단협 추가 비용의 약 75%도 3분기에 선반영
(잔여분은 4분기 반영). 일회성 요인을 제외하면 이익 체력은 견조. 
 동사의 조선 부문 매출액은 4조 3,988억원(+44.0%yoy), 영업이익은 
6,463억원(+62.8%yoy, OPM 14.7%)을 기록. 상선 OPM은 14.7%로 
2Q26(17.8%) 대비 하락했으나 대부분 외생변수 영향. 3Q26 대형선 매출 
중 2023년 수주분 비중은 45%, 2024~2025년 수주분은 51%. 고선가 
물량의 매출 비중이 높아지는 2027년까지 수익성 개선 여력은 충분.  
 9월 말 누적 상선 수주는 138.0억달러로 연간 목표의 120%, 엔진기계는 
41.4억달러로 155%를 달성했으나 함정(13%)과 해양(5%)은 부진. LNG선
은 중국 조선소의 저가 수주와 발주 둔화로 선가가 일부 하락. 선주들이 현 
선가를 저점으로 인식하며 발주 협의를 재개하는 분위기. 북미 LNG 프로젝
트 FID가 이어지고 있어 2027년 LNG선 발주 회복 기대. 
 함정 부문은 매출 2,570억원(-14.6%qoq)으로 줄었으나, 수출 비중 확대에 
따른 믹스 개선으로 영업이익 386억원(+114%qoq, OPM 15.0%)을 기록. 
해양플랜트는 매출액 3,799억원(+35.3%yoy), 영업이익 1,265억원(OPM 
33.3%). C/O 180억원을 제외해도 Ruya 공정 본격화와 Trion 고수익 물량
으로 높은 수익성 유지. 2027년 일부 프로젝트 인도로 매출 감소가 예상되
나, LNG선·블록 물량 투입으로 고정비 부담을 완화할 계획. 신규 해상변전
소(OSS) 계약을 앞두고 있으며, 2027년 초 추가 수주 발표도 기대. 
 주목할 부분은 엔진기계. 영업이익 2,299억원(OPM 24.2%)으로 높은 D/F 
엔진 비중을 바탕으로 견조한 수익성 유지. 육상발전용 힘센엔진은 올해   
3GW를 확보하며 2029년까지 계획 캐파 수준의 물량을 이미 채움. 생산 
예상 물량은 2028년 약 1.5GW, 2029년 약 3GW이며, 신규 공장 구축 이
후 연간 생산능력은 약 4GW로 확대 예정. 북미 Hyperscaler와의 직접 협
상이 늘고 있으며, 힘센엔진을 활용한 부유식 발전설비(BMPP)로 FDC 시장
까지 대응 가능. AI 데이터센터 전력 수요가 2028년 이후 새로운 이익 축. 
 미국에서는 Kiewit과 합작법인 설립 계약 체결. 군수지원함 등 지원함 사업
부터 추진하며, 현지 조선소 건설 과정에서 HII에 선박 블록을 공급하는 방
안도 협의 중. 구체적인 투자계획은 2026년 말까지 확정 목표. 국내에서는 
핵추진잠수함 초기 예산이 2027년 반영되며 동사도 사업 참여를 준비 중. 
 동사에 대한 투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 61만원을 제시. 고선가 물량 매출 확
대와 육상발전용 엔진 성장으로 피크아웃을 이겨낼 굴지의 조선사.

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에도 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

TUẦN 02-06/11 [Điểm nhấn thị trường] 
VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -16.26 điểm tương ứng -0.93%, với thanh 
khoản mở rộng

[Nhận định xu hướng] 
Diễn biến phân hóa và biến động mạnh có thể còn tiếp diễn, tuy nhiên khi xu 
hướng tăng ngắn hạn vẫn đang được bảo lưu, chỉ số được kỳ vọng sẽ tiếp tục 
hướng lên thử thách lại các vùng cản phía trên

### Page 2

2

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -16.26 điểm tương ứng -0.93%, với thanh khoản mở 
rộng. Thiết bị dụng cụ Y tế, Tiện ích khí và Bảo hiểm là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt 
nhất trong tuần qua. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị 
-870 tỷ đồng, tập trung ở các mã MBB, VIC và VCB; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều mua ròng 
ở mức 504 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng -2,010 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần qua 
và đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -10,888 hợp đồng. Tự doanh duy trì mua ròng 1,208 hợp 
đồng nhưng đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -4,070 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam có tuần giảm điểm thứ 2 liên tiếp khi VN-Index lùi về kiểm định vùng hỗ trợ 
quanh 1.725 điểm, với phiên cuối tuần có thời điểm giảm hơn 30 điểm trước khi thu hẹp 
đà giảm nhờ lực cầu ở nhóm chứng khoán. Áp lực bán tập trung ở nhóm vốn hóa lớn, đặc 
biệt là bộ đôi Vingroup (VIC, VHM) cùng một số cổ phiếu ngân hàng và công nghệ, khiến 
chỉ số thiếu lực đỡ. Giao dịch của khối ngoại diễn biến trái chiều và giằng co — có phiên 
mua ròng trở lại ở một số mã, nhưng vẫn xen kẽ bán ròng ở nhóm trụ như VHM, VCB, cho 
thấy dòng vốn ngoại chưa xác lập xu hướng rõ ràng. Bối cảnh bên ngoài kém thuận lợi khi 
nhóm cổ phiếu công nghệ tại Mỹ bị bán tháo, kéo theo các chỉ số chính toàn cầu đồng 
loạt lao dốc. Mặc dù vậy, kỳ vọng vào động thái mở kênh hoán đổi ngoại tệ của NHNN để 
hỗ trợ thanh khoản, hạ lãi suất đã giúp tâm lý nhà đầu tư trong nước ổn định và không 
xuất hiện áp lực bán tháo.

Chỉ số VNINDEX

Source: Trading View, KB Securities Vietnam

Ngắn hạn 
(Dưới 3 tháng)

Trung hạn (Từ 3 đến 9 tháng)

Chỉ báo  Nhận định xu hướng Hành động

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX tuần qua hình thành mẫu nến spinning thân nhỏ với bóng trên/dưới khá dài, 
cùng thanh khoản gia tăng, thể hiện sự giằng co mạnh nhưng về tổng thể lại cho thấy diễn 
biến đã cân bằng trở lại sau tuần giảm điểm trước đó. Trên khung ngày, mặc dù trải qua 
các phiên biến động tăng/giảm mạnh đan xen nhưng việc cho phản ứng bật tăng tích cực 
tại quanh đường biên dưới của mẫu hình tam giác đi ngang trung hạn cho thấy tương quan 
vẫn đang nghiêng về chiều hướng tăng. Diễn biến phân hóa và biến động mạnh có thể còn 
tiếp diễn, tuy nhiên khi xu hướng tăng ngắn hạn vẫn đang được bảo lưu, chỉ số được kỳ 
vọng sẽ tiếp tục hướng lên thử thách lại các vùng cản phía trên.

(Ghi chú: NĐT có thể dựa theo nhận định xu hướng thị trường để tham khảo khuyến nghị 
hành động tương ứng trong bảng phía trên) 
Kháng cự xa: 1800 (+-15) 
Kháng cự gần: 1750 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ gần: 1700 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ xa: 1660 (+-15)

### Page 4

4

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận:

Document 342: Downgrade of global equities to a Core allocation

Source PDF: all report pdf/Downgrade of global equities to a Core allocation.pdf
Report source: 渣打銀行 (Market Watch)
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 9
Status: ok

### Page 1

Downgrade of global 
equities to a Core allocation  
Summary 
Event: We are downgrading global equities from Overweight 
to a Neutral/Core allocation. We continue to maintain a 
Neutral allocation to fixed income, Overweight on Gold and 
Underweight on Cash. There are more details in our Global 
Market Outlook publication on 30 October 2026. 
What does the equity downgrade mean?  
This downgrade implies a slightly lower allocation to global 
equities within a diversified portfolio. For our CIO Foundation 
Balanced portfolio, this would imply an equity allocation of 
52.5%, down from 53.2%. We continue to expect positive 
returns for global equities in the coming 6-12 months, 
though equity investors may need to recalibrate expectations 
lower, compared with the 15-20% annualised gains 
experienced in the last few years. Our capital market 
assumptions still suggest a mid-to-high single digit annual 
returns over the next few years, consistent with a transition 
to a mid-cycle bull market. Consensus estimates point to 
16% earnings growth for global equities in 2027, which 
should overcome various headwinds to deliver positive 
returns for equities over the next 12 months. Investors should 
thus, continue to maintain exposure to equities within a 
diversified portfolio. 
In addition, we see a changing playbook to prepare for 
higher volatility, more active management and greater 
diversification, as discussed below. 
What does it mean for regional equities 
positioning? 
Within a global equities portfolio, we remain Overweight US 
and Asia ex-Japan (AxJ) equities, which are expected to 
deliver the highest earnings growth in 2027 amongst the 
major regions. This growth is led by the technology sector, 
which carries the largest weight in both regions. Within the 
diversified equities portfolio, we maintain Core allocations to

53.2% for our CIO Foundation Balanced portfolio

Euro area equities, which offer lower valuations, and Japan 
equities, which is backed by a reflating economy. We are 
Underweight UK equities, where the sector composition 
offers lower growth prospects in 2027. Within the AxJ region, 
we are Overweight Taiwan equities, with attractive structural 
growth driven by its semiconductor industry. We are also 
Overweight India equities, which offers diversified non-tech 
growth drivers. Taiwan and India equities both generate 
attractive returns on equity. We maintain Core allocations to 
China equities, with low valuations, and Korea equities, with 
explosive earnings growth in its semiconductors industry, but 
volatile price movements. We are Underweight ASEAN, 
which offers relatively lower earnings growth. 
What are the implications for equity sectors? 
We are Overweight these sectors globally: US 
information technology, US healthcare, US financials, US 
materials, Europe ex-UK financials, and Japan 
financials. 
A framework we use is to break the market into four key 
sector building blocks. Tech is the largest block, 
encompassing the Information Technology, Communication 
Services and Consumer Discretionary sectors. Put 
differently, the segment spans five major industries: 
semiconductors, hardware, internet, software, and IT 
services. Within the US market, we expect this segment to 
deliver c.30% earnings growth in 2027, extending the strong 
momentum seen in 2026. 
Financials and Real Estate are the next building block, 
comprising these two sectors. We expect this segment to 
continue delivering low-teens earnings growth in 2027, 
supported by resilient economic activity and healthy credit 
trends. 
Resources include companies across four sectors: 
Industrials, Materials, Energy, and Utilities. We expect the 
segment to generate 10-15% earnings growth in 2027, 
although Energy is likely to remain the largest drag on overall 
growth due to tougher comparisons and softer energy price 
assumptions for 2027. 
Healthcare & Others consists primarily of Healthcare and 
Consumer Staples. We expect mid-teen earnings growth for 
the segment in 2027, with a rebound in healthcare earnings 
representing a potential upside surprise. 
Our Overweight sector exposures are drawn from each of the 
four key blocks above. Unlike the last few years, with 
concentrated gains from Tech providing market leadership, 
we see our downgrade in global equities also reflecting a 
broadening in market leadership towards each of the other 
sector blocks apart from Tech.

As structural themes such as AI transition from the rapid 
buildout phase towards monetisation, diversified exposure 
across sectors and regions should become increasingly 
important. 
What are the implications for equity 
opportunistic ideas? 
We have opportunistic ideas on Global High-Dividend 
equities, US healthcare, Euro area banks, MSCI 
Switzerland and Japan banks. The selection represents a 
more defensive stance currently, with an average beta of 
below 1 relative to global equities (MSCI AC World), but 
attractive drivers nonetheless, for each of the ideas.  
Global High-Dividend offers higher-income equity allocation 
with historically lower volatility than global equities. US 
healthcare is supported by wider adoption of obesity 
treatments, advances in oncology and innovative medical 
devices, that support earnings growth. Euro area banks 
continue to see steady earnings growth adding to their 
excess capital, which is being returned to shareholders via 
attractive dividend and share buyback yields. Japan banks 
continue to enjoy net margin expansion from BoJ policy 
normalisation, as well as corporate governance 
improvements. MSCI Switzerland is a newly initiated idea for 
investors seeking defensive exposure. Healthcare and 
consumer staples accounts for more than half the index 
weight, providing exposure to relatively resilient demand that 
is more insulated from economic cycles and AI trends. It also 
has a sizeable financial sector supported by steady wealth 
management growth and well-capitalised insurers with 
disciplined underwriting and resilient premium income. 
We see the case for more active management in a mid-
cycle transition, as higher market dispersion should create 
greater opportunities for active sector and stock selection, 
particularly as leadership within equities evolves. 
What are the implications of higher equity 
market volatility?  
A more uncertain political backdrop and potentially maturing 
growth rates in certain market segments may contribute to 
more frequent bouts of market volatility. Higher volatility 
presents an opportunity to use structured strategies to gain 
exposure to equities. Such strategies may be used to 
generate income or to gain exposure at a discounted entry 
price. Investors can also consider strategies that are 
structured to return principal or partial principal at maturity. 
What alternative strategies are suited to a 
regime of moderating equity market returns?  
Investors can consider alternative strategies such as hedge 
fund strategies or private markets, for those with the capacity

to accept some illiquidity.  
Equity long/short hedge fund strategies would retain growth 
participation with less market exposure. Fund managers buy 
companies they expect to outperform and short those they 
expect to underperform, seeking to earn the difference 
between the two, and benefit even as total equity market 
returns are moderating. 
Within private markets, private equity provides a means to 
higher returns with the effects of an illiquidity premium. 
Meanwhile, infrastructure offers differentiated exposure to 
structural growth opportunities, where contracted long-term 
revenues reduce dependence on equity-market sentiment. 
What are the implications on fixed income 
and FX markets? 
Bonds: This year’s surge in bond yields means bonds now 
offer a hedge against any equity market weakness. We 
believe the 3-7-year part of the US bond yield curve offers 
the best hedge against any equity sell-off caused by AI-
related growth worries. This part of the bond curve has priced 
in most Fed rate hike expectations, which could reverse if 
growth concerns return. Meanwhile, we favour US Treasury 
Inflation-Protected Securities (TIPS) as a hedge against any 
fuel price-led inflation scare.  
Credit: For corporate bonds, adopting a defensive stance in 
a volatile environment typically involves: i) increasing credit 
quality, favouring higher-rated issuers; and ii) moving up the 
capital structure, by reducing exposure to subordinated debt 
and favouring secured debt. From a sector perspective, 
large, diversified banks, utilities, and quasi-sovereigns tend 
to be more defensive, particularly relative to cyclical sectors, 
such as metals and mining, exploration, and technology.         
FX: We favour bearish AUD/JPY as a tactical hedge against 
any equity market correction. Review of 21 past S&P500 
correction sessions (2010-2025) shows AUD weakened 
against USD in 86% of cases, while JPY strengthened 
against USD in 67%, supporting our bearish AUD/JPY 
outlook during any equity market stress.

Document 360: Following the oil shock, El Niño threatens to hurt macroeconomic resilience in Sub-Saharan Africa further (2026-10-21)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Following the oil shock, El Niño threatens to hurt macroeconomic resilience in Sub-Saharan Africa further (2026-10-21).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-21
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

21 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
FOLLOWING THE OIL SHOCK, EL NIÑO THREATENS TO HURT MACROECONOMIC RESILIENCE IN SUB-SAHARAN
AFRICA FURTHER
Lucas Plé
Seven months after the start of the war in the Middle East, which has led to a sharp rise in oil prices, sub-Saharan Africa is holding up well
overall. The deterioration in external accounts and the rise in inflation are more contained than in 2022, during the last oil shock. However,
this resilience is fragile and is eroding as the conflict drags on. Furthermore, an exceptional El Niño event could significantly worsen the

macroeconomic environment in 2027 1.
Economic growth remains robust
For countries with available data, GDP growth held up well in the first half of 2026 2. In mid-October, the IMF revised its regional forecast for
the full year upwards to 4.5% (+0.2 percentage points compared with April), in line with the performance for 2025.
1 El Niño 2026–2027: multiple pressures on prices and supplies
2 1.4% year-on-year (y/y) in South Africa, 4.2% in Nigeria, 5.1% in Kenya, 7% in Angola, 7.3% in Ghana.

Sub-Saharan Africa is highly vulnerable to climate shocks via the agricultural commodities channel

### Page 2

2
The bank for
a changing
world
Chart of the Week / 21 October 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
A mitigated shock on terms of trade
High prices for export commodities (gold, copper, platinum) have helped to cushion the impact on terms of trade and the external
accounts of hydrocarbon-importing countries. For the 10 countries in our sample (see heatmap), the depreciation of their currencies
against the US dollar since last February has been moderate overall (2.3% on average).

Inflation has started to rise again, but it is still being contained so far
In the ten countries in our sample, although inflation is rising again, it is lower year-on-year for the first eight months of 2026 than it was
for the same period in 2022. This is due to:
1. Monetary policies that were still restrictive before the conflict started;
2. Fuel subsidies;
3.   Macroeconomic reforms implemented in recent years (a return to orthodox monetary policy in Nigeria, and efforts towards fiscal

consolidation in many countries);
4. Moderate currency depreciation since February.
As a result, few central banks have tightened their monetary policy since February (South Africa, Uganda and Tanzania).
However, this resilience is still fragile…
On the one hand, governments that have chosen to cap fuel price rises are seeing their public finances deteriorate. In Senegal, energy
subsidies will go above 3% of GDP in 2026, which is unsustainable. On the other hand, the region’s external accounts are exposed to

volatile global commodity prices and tighter international financing conditions.
… and the macroeconomic environment could quickly worsen in 2027, due to an exceptional El Niño event
The region is particularly vulnerable to climate shocks via the agricultural commodities channel. Given agriculture’s high share in its GDP,
sub-Saharan Africa is more exposed to downside risks to growth than Latin America, Asia or Central Europe (1 st column of the heatmap).

It is also more exposed than other regions of the world to the inflationary effect of rising food prices, given their higher share in the
consumer basket (2 nd column). Finally, even though its dependence on food imports is lower than in Asia and Europe (3 rd column), the
region is highly vulnerable to rising international food commodity prices due to its fragile external accounts. For several countries in our
sample, foreign exchange reserves cover less than four months’ worth of imports of goods and services (Uganda, Tanzania, Ghana and

Zambia), leaving little room for manoeuvre for absorbing a further external shock.
In addition to shocks via the agricultural commodities channel, other localised risks must be considered. In Southern Africa, El Niño is
expected to intensify droughts and threaten electricity generation as a result, particularly in Zambia (where hydropower accounts
for nearly 80% of domestic electricity generation). In East Africa, the increased flooding that El Niño is expected to cause will threaten

infrastructure – particularly logistics infrastructure – which is already less efficient than the global average 3. These two sources of
vulnerability could also increase pressure on growth, prices and the external accounts.
In conclusion, if oil prices remain high and El Niño is exceptionally severe, the region will struggle to absorb this double shock in 2027.
Despite their status as net exporters of hydrocarbons, Nigeria and Angola are also exposed to a deterioration in the macroeconomic

environment in 2027. Only South Africa appears to be better positioned to absorb the shock.
lucas.ple@bnpparibas.com
3 See the World Bank’s Logistics Performance Index.

2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
TODAY’S INSIGHTS
Financial Markets
• Risk sentiment softened into the end of the week as
renewed selling in tech stocks weighed on equities. The
moves followed last week’s turmoil at Nvidia, although a
softer-than-expected US producer price report provided

some offset by tempering expectations for aggressive Fed
rate hikes.
• Key US equity indices finished Friday mostly lower, led by
the tech sector. The S&P 500 declined 0.4% to 7,562 but
finished the week 2.5% higher. The tech-heavy Nasdaq
underperformed, falling 0.9% on the day, although it ended
the week 3.5% higher. The Dow Jones Industrial Average
gained 0.1% and finished the week around 2.0% higher.
• European equities were mixed on Friday but ended the week

firmly in the green, underpinned by lower oil prices earlier
in the week and solid earnings from heavyweights. The
Euro Stoxx 50 fell 0.2% on Friday but ended the week 3.5%
higher, while Germany’s DAX was unchanged on the day
but 3.6% higher over the week. The FTSE 100 rose 0.4% on
the day and finished the week 2.1% higher. Asian markets
were mixed, with the Nikkei rising 0.5% and the Hang Seng
losing 1.6%. Locally, the S&P/ASX 200 fell 0.7% to 9,082.

Equity futures point to a broadly flat open, with the SPI 200
unchanged at 9,114.
• Outside the US, bond yields generally moved higher. In the
US, bonds rallied on the back of the softer-than-expected
producer price index and some dovish Fed speak. The yield
on the policy-sensitive two-year US Treasury fell 4bps to
4.84%, while the 10-year Treasury yield increased 1bp to
5.14%. Traders are now pricing around 25bps of rate hikes
by the end of 2026 and almost 60bps by the end of 2027.

Germany’s 10-year Bund yield rose 3bps to 3.38%, UK
10-year gilt yields increased 4bps to 5.34%, and Japanese
10-year government bond yields rose 6bps to 2.87%.
• Australian bond yields moved higher. The 3-year
government bond yield rose 7bps to 4.79%, while the
10-year yield increased 8bps to 5.12%. In futures trading,
the 3-year government futures yield rose a further 1bp to
4.79%, while the 10-year futures yield increased 2bps to
5.15%. OIS markets expect the RBA Board to remain on

hold through much of 2027. Traders are pricing almost no
chance of a December hike and around 7bps of tightening
at the February 2027 meeting, before pricing shifts towards
the possibility of rate cuts later in 2027. The labour force
survey later this week will be closely watched and is likely
to influence market pricing.
• In foreign exchange markets, the US dollar strengthened
modestly, with the DXY rising 0.2% to 103.41. The
Australian dollar fell 0.5% against the US dollar to

US$0.6772, while the trade-weighted index declined 0.5%
to 63.3. Against the major crosses, the Australian dollar fell
0.6% against the yen, 0.4% against sterling, 0.1% against
the New Zealand dollar, 0.2% against the euro, and 0.4%
against both the Canadian and Singapore dollars.
• Commodity markets were generally weaker. The CRB Index
fell 0.5%, while WTI crude oil declined 0.2% to US$88.56
a barrel. Gold edged 0.2% higher to US$3,884.49/oz, while

copper fell 1.4%. Coking coal declined 0.4% to US$252.00/t
and iron ore fell 0.5% to US$90.00/t. Thermal coal bucked
the broader trend, rising 0.3% to US$145.75/t, while ACCU
prices fell 4.3% to $36.13.
International Data
US PPI slowed in October from 1.4% to 0.3%. Excluding food
and energy, momentum softened from 0.8% to 0.3%.
New York and Chicago Fed Presidents Williams and Goolsbee
kept the focus on inflation pressures and upside price risks
on Friday, and remained of the view that further tightening is

necessary. As for other recent Fed speakers, the labour market
is not currently a concern.
Euro Area GDP’s second estimate for Q3 met expectations,
unrevised from the flash estimate at 0.4%, 1.3%yr.
The Euro Area trade deficit widened at the margin in
September from a revised EUR7.8bn to EUR8.7bn.
Local Data
Westpac Economics released its November Market Outlook,
with updated domestic and global forecasts (see here).
We reaffirm our view that the RBA’s tightening cycle is over,

with our Chief Economist highlighting greater conviction in the
call (see here).

Document 417: SemiAnalysis大幅下修AI算力预测：2028年资本开支预测下调近三成，五年硅片出货面积下调17%，英伟达受冲击最严重

Source PDF: all report pdf/SemiAnalysis大幅下修AI算力预测：2028年资本开支预测下调近三成，五年硅片出货面积下调17%，英伟达受冲击最严重.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-16
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

SemiAnalysis 将 2028 年全球 AI 加速器资本开⽀预测下调 28% ， 2030 年下修 40% ； 2026—
2030 年加速器硅⽚出货⾯积累计预测下调 17% ， 2028 年 HBM 需求预测更被砍掉 30% ，
2030 年功耗需求预测下调 34% 。英伟达成为最⼤受冲击者，占全球硅⽚出货削减幅度的
66% ，其 2030 年按计算收⼊测算的出货预测下调幅度⾼达 66% 。
摘要：
AI 基础设施投资热潮出现新的降温信号。
半导体研究机构 SemiAnalysis 在 11 ⽉ 13 ⽇发布的最新模型更新中，⼤幅下调了未来数年 AI 加速
器出货、资本开⽀、 HBM 需求和电⼒需求预测。其中，2028 年⾄ 2030 年的调整幅度尤为明显：
2028 年全球 AI 加速器资本开⽀预测被下调 28% ， 2029 年和 2030 年分别下调 41% 和 40% 。
按照最新模型， 2030 年全球 AI 加速器资本开⽀将从此前预测的 1.531 万亿美元降⾄ 9230 亿美

元，减少 6080 亿美元。与此同时，2026 年⾄ 2030 年加速器逻辑硅⽚出货⾯积累计预测下调
17% ，英伟达占据其中 66% 的削减幅度。
在这轮预测调整中，英伟达受到的冲击最⼤。SemiAnalysis 不仅⼤幅下调了英伟达未来数年的
出货预测，还进⼀步调低了新⼀代产品的平均售价假设，认为随着 AI 硬件市场竞争加剧，英伟
达需要接受更低的利润率来维持竞争⼒。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

AI 资本开⽀预期骤降， 2030 年预测下修 6080 亿美元
SemiAnalysis 此次调整最直观的信号，是对全球 AI 加速器资本开⽀规模的重新评估。
根据其最新模型， 2026 年全球 AI 加速器资本开⽀预测基本没有变化，仍在 3920 亿美元左右。但
从 2027 年开始，原有的⾼增⻓预期明显下修。
2027 年资本开⽀预测从此前的 7700 亿美元降⾄ 6270 亿美元，下调 19% ； 2028 年从 1.054 万亿美
元降⾄ 7580 亿美元，下调 28% ； 2029 年从 1.352 万亿美元降⾄ 7960 亿美元，下调 41% ； 2030 年
则从 1.531 万亿美元降⾄ 9230 亿美元，下调 40% 。
这意味着， SemiAnalysis 此前预期的 AI 加速器资本开⽀持续⾼速扩张路径，正在被明显修正。
尤其是 2029 年和 2030 年，原本超过 1 万亿美元的年度投资规模，如今被下调⾄ 8000 亿⾄ 9000 亿
美元区间。
这并不意味着 AI 基础设施投资将停⽌增⻓。按照新预测，全球 AI 加速器资本开⽀仍将从 2026 年

的 3920 亿美元增⻓⾄ 2030 年的 9230 亿美元，只是增⻓规模明显低于此前预期。
资本开⽀下修背后，是 AI 产业收⼊增⻓和融资环境的双重变化。
SemiAnalysis 指出，前沿 AI 实验室第三季度新增年度经常性收⼊（ ARR ）增速放缓，促使其重
新审视整个⽣态系统能够产⽣的 Token 收⼊规模。为了让 AI 业务收⼊能够更加持续地覆盖计算成
本，该机构相应下调了未来算⼒供给的扩张预期。
与此同时，债务和股权投资者对 AI 基础设施资本开⽀的投资意愿正在减弱，尤其是缺乏⼤型云服
务商（ Hyperscaler ）承诺采购⽀持的项⽬。这将直接影响数据中⼼⼟地、电⼒和建筑主体
（ Shell ）的开发，导致未来可⽤于部署 AI 计算系统的电⼒供应减少。
美国中期选举结果则进⼀步加剧了这⼀压⼒。 SemiAnalysis 预计，原计划于 2027 年下半年⾄
2028 年上半年投产的美国数据中⼼供给中，有 5.8GW 将被推迟，监管因素也将进⼀步压缩 2028
年的数据中⼼容量。

不过，该机构特别强调，中期选举带来的数据中⼼建设延迟只是预测下修的额外因素， AI 收⼊增
⻓放缓和融资环境收紧才是更重要的驱动因素。
硅⽚出货⾯积累计下调 17% ， HBM 需求也遭⼤幅修正
资本开⽀预测下修之外， SemiAnalysis 对实际硬件需求的判断同样趋于谨慎。
按照最新模型，2026 年⾄ 2030 年全球 AI 加速器逻辑硅⽚出货⾯积累计预测下调 17% 。其中，
2027 年、 2028 年、 2029 年和 2030 年的预测分别下调 13% 、 16% 、 24% 和 15% 。
值得注意的是，英伟达占据了硅⽚出货⾯积削减幅度的 66% ，意味着本轮 AI 硬件需求预期调整
并⾮各家供应商平均承受压⼒，英伟达是最主要的受影响者。
HBM 需求预测也出现明显下修。 2028 年全球 HBM 出货量预测从此前的 9.0EB 降⾄ 6.4EB ，下调
30% ； 2029 年从 10.3EB 降⾄ 7.9EB ，下调 23% ； 2030 年从 10.9EB 降⾄ 9.6EB ，下调 12% 。

### Page 3

SemiAnalysis 认为，HBM 供需平衡正在改善，因此下调了未来 HBM 价格假设，并将价格变化传
导⾄各类 AI 芯⽚的物料成本（ BOM ）。
此前， 2027 年 AI 基础设施⽀出的相当⼀部分增⻓来⾃价格上涨，尤其是存储器价格。此次价格
预测下调，在该机构看来，也意味着供应链正在回归更加正常的状态。
此外， AI 加速器功耗需求也被⼤幅下修。按照最新预测， 2030 年 AI 加速器功耗需求将从此前的
122.8GW 降⾄ 80.7GW ，下调 34% ； 2029 年预测从 108.7GW 降⾄ 72.5GW ，下调 33% 。
这⼀变化既来⾃芯⽚出货量减少，也来⾃未来单颗芯⽚功耗假设的调整。 SemiAnalysis 指出，
电⼒持续成为限制因素，未来加速器产品路线图中的最⾼芯⽚功耗也可能趋于温和。
英伟达承受最⼤压⼒，未来芯⽚出货预测⼤幅下修
在主要 AI 芯⽚供应商中，英伟达成为此次预测调整中受影响最⼤的公司。
SemiAnalysis 按照计算收⼊（ Compute Revenue ）⼝径，将英伟达 2027 年⾄ 2030 年的出货预

测分别下调 22% 、 39% 、 56% 和 66%；按照包含整个系统在内的总功耗⼝径计算，下调幅度分
别为 14% 、 35% 、 50% 和 59% 。
价格⽅⾯， SemiAnalysis 不仅考虑了 HBM 成本下降带来的影响，还认为英伟达在 Feynman 及后
续产品上需要降低加价幅度，以应对⽇益激烈的竞争。
具体来看， Feynman 的平均售价预测从约 11 万美元下调⾄约 7.8 万美元， Feynman Ultra 从约
17.5 万美元下调⾄约 9.8 万美元， Feynman Next 则从约 19.1 万美元下调⾄约 11.8 万美元。
出货量和售价双双下调，将对英伟达未来收⼊预测形成更⼤压⼒。此次调整涉及的不仅是
GPU ，还包括 Groq LPU 和独⽴ CPU 产品。
SemiAnalysis 解释称，英伟达受到更⼤冲击，主要是因为其芯⽚在部分⼤型 AI 实验室的算⼒采
购组合中，相较其他平台的吸引⼒下降。
以 Anthropic 为例，其使⽤的四⼤计算平台包括 TPU 、 Trainium 、 AMD GPU 和英伟达 GPU 。

SemiAnalysis 认为，从性能成本⽐来看，英伟达 GPU 在这四类平台中处于边际位置， Anthropic
更倾向于其他平台。

### Page 4

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
在 OpenAI ⽅⾯，尽管 GPU 在其基础设施中仍占据较重要地位，但 AMD GPU 已经成为颇具吸引
⼒的选择。此外， OpenAI 还将优先部署 Jalapeno 和下⼀代 Serrano 等⾃研 ASIC 项⽬。
新型云计算服务商（ Neocloud ）则构成英伟达的另⼀项⻛险。 SemiAnalysis 指出，英伟达对
Neocloud 客⼾的敞⼝最⾼，尤其是规模较⼩的⻓尾客⼾。这类企业⽬前⾯临更⼤的项⽬执⾏和
融资⻛险。
值得注意的是， SemiAnalysis 强调，此次模型调整并未纳⼊⻩仁勋辞任英伟达 CEO 可能产⽣的
影响，即使⻩仁勋继续担任 CEO ，该机构也会作出相同的预测下修。不过，该机构认为，领导
层变动可能进⼀步增加英伟达市场份额流失的下⾏⻛险。
博通相对稳健， AMD 和联发科⾯临不同程度调整
与英伟达形成鲜明对⽐的是，博通在此次预测调整中表现出更强的韧性。
SemiAnalysis 将博通 2026 年⾄ 2030 年 ASIC 业务累计收⼊预测下调 11% ，主要原因是 HBM 成本

下降带来的平均售价调整，⽽⾮⼤规模削减出货量。
该机构认为，博通的主要 ASIC 项⽬，包括⾕歌 TPU 、 OpenAI 和 Anthropic 相关项⽬，在性能成
本⽐和能效⽅⾯具备优势，同时博通还能够通过与债权⼈合作，为数据中⼼⼟地、电⼒、建筑
主体和系统部署提供融资⽀持。
AMD 同样展现出⼀定韧性，但仍⾯临明显下修。 SemiAnalysis 按照总功耗⼝径，将 AMD 2028
年⾄ 2030 年的预测分别下调 23% 、 55% 和 57% ；按照⽀出⼝径，下调幅度分别为 29% 、 35% 和
32% 。 2027 年预测则相对稳定，功耗⼝径基本持平，⽀出⼝径下调 11% 。
AMD 受到的冲击⼩于英伟达，主要得益于其在 Meta 、 OpenAI 和 Anthropic 等⼤型客⼾中的布
局。SemiAnalysis 认为， Meta 和 OpenAI 与 AMD 之间的认股权证交易安排，使采购 AMD 系统具
有较强吸引⼒； Anthropic 也将 AMD 视为推理计算领域性能成本⽐仅次于 TPU 的选择。

此外， AMD 近期产品部署节奏反⽽有所提前。 SemiAnalysis 将约 2.5 万颗 MI400 芯⽚和约 3 万颗
Meta 定制 MI450-MC 芯⽚的出货时间，从 2027 年第四季度提前⾄ 2026 年第四季度，以反映更强
劲的⽣产爬坡。
联发科则⾯临更明显的出货量调整。 SemiAnalysis 将 Humufish ⽣命周期出货量在 2028 年⾄ 2029
年累计下调 47 万颗，并将⾯向 Anthropic 的 Triggerfish 项⽬⽣命周期出货量下调 42% 。
亚⻢逊 Trainium 则是此次调整中少数近端预测未被下调的平台。SemiAnalysis 没有削减
Trainium 在 2027 年和 2028 年的出货预测，原因是亚⻢逊拥有较为充⾜的数据中⼼项⽬储备，且
Trainium 制造成本更低，亚⻢逊也愿意通过战略补贴⽀持其关键客⼾。
整体来看， SemiAnalysis 此次调整并不意味着 AI 算⼒需求将全⾯消失，⽽是意味着此前建⽴在
持续融资、数据中⼼快速扩张和⾼价硬件基础上的增⻓预期正在重新校准。

当 AI 实验室收⼊增速放缓、资本市场融资收紧，芯⽚供应商之间的竞争也将从单纯的性能⽐拼，
进⼀步转向性能成本⽐、能效和融资能⼒的综合较量。
英伟达仍然拥有强⼤的 AI 芯⽚市场地位，但在 SemiAnalysis 看来，其未来增⻓将⾯临⽐部分
ASIC 供应商更⼤的压⼒。

### Page 5

市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

Document 426: The Commodities Feed_ Metals under pressure while oil supply improves

Source PDF: all report pdf/The Commodities Feed_ Metals under pressure while oil supply improves.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-17
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

Oil remains under pressure as Middle East supply recovers, while
aluminium tightness keeps nearby spreads elevated. Copper
and iron ore continue to face pressure from a softer demand
backdrop

Ewa Manthey and Warren Patterson
Brent is trading below $92/bbl as improving supply conditions outweigh
continued geopolitical risks in the Middle East
Energy - Oil extends decline as supply
concerns ease
Oil prices extended their decline for a second session, with Brent
trading below $92/bbl as improving supply conditions outweighed
continued geopolitical risks in the Middle East. The physical picture
has improved considerably in recent months. Crude oil and fuel
ﬂows through the Strait of Hormuz rose to 9.1mb/d in 3Q26 from
5.1mb/d in the previous quarter. However, ﬂows remain well below
the 21.4mb/d recorded a year earlier, highlighting that regional
supply has yet to fully normalise, as data from the Energy
Information Administration (EIA) shows.

### Page 2

Other indicators also point to easing near-term tightness. Brent’s
prompt spread has narrowed to around $2.40/bbl in backwardation
from more than $3/bbl a month ago. Meanwhile, speculative
positioning has continued to weaken, with money managers
cutting their net long in NYMEX WTI by 14,719 lots to 70,584 lots
last week, marking a seventh consecutive weekly decline.
Geopolitical risks remain elevated, with continued attacks on
vessels highlighting the vulnerability of regional export

infrastructure. However, improving ﬂows from the Persian Gulf and
expectations for a looser global oil balance are increasingly
oﬀsetting these concerns. We expect improving supply availability
to remain a headwind for oil prices, although any renewed
disruption to ﬂows through the Strait of Hormuz remains the key
upside risk.
Elsewhere, US natural gas prices extended their decline as
improving supply and forecasts for warmer weather weighed on
the market. Production in the Appalachian region appears to be

recovering as producers bring previously withheld volumes back to
the market, while above-average temperatures forecast for parts of
the Midwest and Northeast should reduce near-term heating
demand. However, strong LNG demand should provide some
support, with ﬂows to US export terminals reaching record levels
and limiting the amount of gas available to the domestic market.
Metals - Gold rebounds from recent lows
Gold edged higher this morning after falling to its lowest close since

September 2025 in the previous session. The modest rebound
comes after a sharp sell-oﬀ driven by persistent US inﬂation, higher
Treasury yields and growing expectations that the Federal Reserve
will keep monetary policy tighter for longer. Weakness in
technology stocks has also weighed on bullion recently as investors
reduced exposure to cover losses elsewhere. Still, the prospect of
further Fed tightening remains a headwind for precious metals.
Attention now turns to the minutes from the Fed’s October meeting

for further clues on the outlook for interest rates. We expect gold to

In base metals, copper is trading around its lowest level in more
than four months as weakness in technology stocks and softer
Chinese economic data has weighed on sentiment. The sell-oﬀ in
AI-related shares has raised concerns around the demand outlook
for copper, given the metal’s growing exposure to investment in
power grids and data centres.

Weaker Chinese industrial and production data yesterday added to
concerns over demand from the world’s largest copper consumer.
However, signs of tightness remain in China’s domestic market,
where near-term contracts are trading at a sizeable premium to
later-dated prices amid depleted inventories and constrained
availability.
Meanwhile, aluminium’s nearby market has tightened sharply, with
the Tom/next spread rising above $115/t and spot metal
commanding a premium of more than $250/t over the three-

month contract. Low exchange inventories have left the market
particularly sensitive to demand for prompt metal, pushing the
front of the LME curve deeper into backwardation. We expect this
tightness to continue supporting prices in the near term. Further
out, however, the supply picture looks less supportive as stronger
production growth should gradually improve availability and
loosen the global balance next year.

end of the year and the gradual ramp-up of the Simandou mine in
Guinea. China’s crude steel output fell further in October to its

lowest level since early 2025, reinforcing concerns over demand for
steelmaking raw materials. While seasonal restocking ahead of

Document 434: Three Questions About China’s Hidden Foreign Exchange Assets (2026-11-12)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Three Questions About China’s Hidden Foreign Exchange Assets (2026-11-12).pdf
Report source: CFR (Follow the Money)
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 17
Status: ok

### Page 1

1
EXPERT TAKE FOLLOW THE MONEY
Three Questions About China’s Hidden Foreign
Exchange Assets
A trillion-dollar gap in foreign exchange purchases raises questions about where
China’s foreign currency assets are held.
Carlos Rawlins/Reuters
Articles Follow the Money Share
Log In
Regions Topics Research Experts Events The Future of American Strategy

### Page 2

2
PUBLISHED
November 11, 2026 8:32 p.m.
Brad W. Setser CFR EXPERT
Whitney Shepardson Senior Fellow
The PBOC argues—unpersuasively, I think—that it
doesn’t have any hidden foreign exchange reserves. I
would reframe that as hidden foreign currency assets,
as foreign currency lent out (or swapped out) with
domestic financial institutions is a classic way of
masking reserves.
Many in Asia—and some in the IMF—are inclined
to believe the PBOC. Fair enough, it is hard to

challenge an institution as large and powerful (in
some ways) as the PBOC.

### Page 3

3
But to convince, the PBOC really needs to provide
answers to three questions.
1. What explains the current gap between foreign
exchange settlement and the PBOC’s reserves?
Settlement is the purchase of foreign exchange in the
market by the PBOC and the state banks. It
technically implies taking an open foreign currency
position, as the foreign exchange is being bought with
renminbi.
Historically, it correlated with changes in the PBOC’s
balance sheet.

And some gaps before 2012 can be explained. In late
2005 and 2006, the PBOC did over $100 billion in
swaps with the state banks, who used the FX to buy
foreign bonds (portfolio debt outflows surged). Those

### Page 4

4
swaps were unwound after the global financial crisis,
and actually provided some of the foreign currency
that “funded” the China Investment Corporation
when it initially scaled up.
And in 2007 and 2008, the PBOC required that the
state banks hold FX against their domestic RMB
deposits (to keep the FX out of reserves; it was held
on deposit at the PBOC and shows up as “other
foreign assets” on the PBOC’s balance sheet and as
“other, other, short-term” in the balance of payments.*

After 2018, though, there are unexplained gaps
between the FX bought in settlement and the
PBOC’s balance sheet (technically cumulative
monthly changes to capture the fact that FX is held as
its initial CNY purchase price).
There was a net accumulation of FX outside the
PBOC’s balance sheet in 2020 and 2021, when the
CNY was under pressure to appreciate. There are
modest sales in 2023 and 2024 when there is
depreciation pressure. And there are large purchases

in 2025 and the first 9 months of 2026—total
purchases in settlement in the last 12 months (spot)
are $500 to 600 billion, while the increase in the
PBOC’s balance sheet is only around $50 billion over
the same period.

### Page 5

5
The gap over time is now $1 trillion. The PBOC
needs to be able to answer a simple question: who
holds this FX, and who holds the associated FX
market risk (given the FX has been purchased with
RMB).
The monthly pattern of growth in settlement strongly
suggests it is backdoor intervention; there are sales
when spot is weak (economically) relative to the fix,
and purchases when spot is either at the fix or just
stronger than the fix (the strong edge of the band

hasn’t been used). The pattern of purchases and sales
thus looks exactly like that of a central bank
maintaining a tight band around a (crawling) peg.
If the PBOC cannot credibly explain this gap, there
isn’t much more to discuss. The PBOC argues that it
hasn’t swapped FX with the state banks or put foreign
currency on deposit at the state banks. Fair enough.

### Page 6

6
But someone is buying in settlement; the PBOC
ought to know who…
2. Missing interest income
There is an obvious tell here—Bloomberg reported
back in 2022 that the PBOC transferred $158 billion
in accumulated interest income to the Ministry of
Finance as part of a special dividend to hold the
budget deficit down. Again, fair enough. But
absolutely nothing changed in the FX reserves held
on the PBOC’s balance sheet or in the balance of
payments.
That highlights a broader issue. Since the end of

2016, the PBOC’s balance sheet FX hasn’t really
changed.

### Page 7

7
Peer institutions generally have an upward slope to
their reserves as a result of “gains from reserve
management” or “reinvestment of interest income.” **
The Central Bank of China (Taipei) and Japan’s
Ministry of Finance are good examples.
The interest on ~$3 trillion in reserves should be
around $100 billion a year. A bit less during the “low-
for-long” era before the pandemic, when SAFE wasn’t
getting much income out of its euro portfolio. A bit

more now, when U.S. short-term rates are well above 3
percent and SAFE’s legacy agencies carry yields of 5
or 6 percent. $100 billion over 10 years is real money
—roughly a trillion dollars.
If SAFE has steadily been selling its interest income,
it should say so (the fact that BOP reserves have often
diverged from the PBOC balance sheet suggests a
more complex story).

### Page 8

8
Moreover, if SAFE has been transferring the FX to
one of China’s many sovereign wealth funds
(including those controlled by SAFE) or the policy
banks, it should say so.
But SAFE cannot say what you see is what we really
have if it cannot explain how it handles the interest
on its now $3.5 trillion in reserves.
3. The accounting for the PBOC’s entrusted loans
and other foreign currency claims on domestic
financial institutions, notably the policy banks

FX handed over to a sovereign wealth fund to manage
doesn’t appear in reserves. It isn’t a liquid reserve asset.
But it should appear elsewhere in the balance of
payments as either portfolio debt or portfolio equity.
And FX handed over to the policy banks through
mechanisms such as entrusted loans (or a simple
deposit or swap) doesn’t count as a foreign reserve—
the foreign currency claim is on a domestic financial
institution, and that isn’t considered a “foreign” asset.

There is no doubt that the PBOC has domestic
claims on China’s financial institutions. The use of
“entrusted” loans to fund the policy banks in 2010
was reported by Caixin and the Financial Times.

### Page 9

9
$95 billion in entrusted loans were converted into
equity in the CDB and China Exim back in 2015
(giving SAFE majority equity stakes in both
institutions through its Buttonwood investment
vehicle).
And the SAFE’s 2020 annual report contained a ten-
year progress report on the “diversified use” of China’s
FX (basically, the non-reserve use) which indicated
that SAFE continued to provide entrusted loans to
small, medium, and large Chinese financial

institutions.
Now perhaps these instruments have been unwound
—the China Development Corporation (per Stephen
Green, a friend) has reduced its foreign currency
balance sheet over the last few years. But that should
lead the foreign currency to flow back to SAFE
absent another use—and that increase in FX isn’t
showing up in the PBOC’s reserves.
In other words, SAFE should provide a transparent
accounting of the FX that has been bought but
transferred to other financial institutions and any

ongoing foreign currency exposure to the domestic
banking system (including the policy banks).
These aren’t big asks. The settlement gap is big and
obvious, interest income is a known issue, and SAFE
itself has reported its use of entrusted loans in the
past. There is no issue around the past use of swaps

### Page 10

10
either. A substantial share of the banks’ foreign
currency assets are funded domestically. It should be
possible for SAFE to explain the role, if any, it has
played in this funding.
There is, of course, another issue around China’s
balance of payments: the growth in foreign currency
external assets funded out of domestic foreign
currency deposits.
China argues that the growing foreign assets of its
state banks are simply a natural part of a growing

domestic financial system. But there is more to the
story.
Onshore FX deposits have tended to rise when
economics say they should not, and fall when the
economics say they should (offshore FX deposits
behave as expected). In 2020 and 2021, FX deposits

### Page 11

11
rose even when there was more yield on RMB
deposits.
And RMB deposits have continued to rise in 2025
and 2026 even with the RMB’s appreciation
offsetting the higher dollar deposit rate. This isn’t an
accident: banks just raised the interest rate that they
pay on dollar deposits to encourage exporters NOT to
convert their export proceeds into RMB and thus
take pressure off the PBOC (per Reuters).
In other words, the pattern of the banks’ foreign

currency asset growth suggests that they are offsetting
appreciation pressure by bidding for FX (or receiving
it from other state actors), not that FX flows into the
banks during periods when onshore residents worry
about depreciation.

### Page 12

12
Right now, the “settlement gap” is the most pressing
issue. FX deposit growth in excess of FX loan growth
can explain FX outflows of around $150 billion in the
four quarters to Q3. The FX outflows implied by
settlement reached $600 billion over the last four
quarters. Together they account for the bulk of
China’s reported $825 billion plus (I estimate a Q4
2025 to Q3 2026 surplus of around $900 billion) in
outflows implied by the reported current account.***

### Page 13

13
It goes without saying that this matters because the
weak RMB is the central reason for China’s massive
export outperformance since 2023—and that export
outperformance has continued in 2026.****
* I have been doing this a long time; I actually do
know most of the historical quirks in China’s balance

### Page 14

14
of payments data.
** The MoF can count a portion of its unrepatriated
gains from accumulated interest on its large reserves
against the budget deficit, but since the actual
interest is held abroad, it still needs to borrow
domestically against those reserves (domestic JGB
issuance and foreign reserves both go up).
*** My estimate of a larger “true” current account
comes from moving gold imports over to the financial
account, and from an assumption that undercounting

of the reinvested earnings of funds held offshore
explains the obvious problem in the investment
income account.
**** The spike in imports in Q1 wasn’t entirely real.
China was slow to adjust its import price index for
the rise in semiconductor/chip prices. This inflated
both import and export volumes in Q1. The rise in
gold imports this year also registers in the import
volume series—though the rise is best understood as
“importing” a financial asset.

### Page 15

15
This work represents the views solely of the author(s). The
Council on Foreign Relations is an independent,
nonpartisan membership organization, think tank, and
publisher, and takes no institutional positions on matters
of policy.
